or `ENUMERATIREV:		/*  0x000  */
		/*
		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
		 *
		 *  The address where this happens is:
		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
		 *
		 *  4.18 works too.
		 *
		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
		 */
		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
			if (cpu->is_32bit)
				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
			else
				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*
			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
			 */
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
			}
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
				int j;

				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
				    "reboot" command:  */
				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;

				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
			}

			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

			if (idata != 0)
				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
		break;

	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
		// No warning.
		break;

	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
		crime_update_crime_time(d);
		break;

	default:
		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
			    (int)relative_addr, len);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" ]\n");
		} else {
			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
		}
	}

 crime_declined:
	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
	    and is still answered.  */
	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}

```diff
+def POST(A):
+    """A refused lb at +0x07 sets d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN before anything else in the session."""

+        x, _ = st(A, op, CRIME + 0, 0, at=PROBE_PC)
+    else:
+        x, _ = ld(A, op, CRIME + 0, "s0", at=PROBE_PC)
+    c += x + ["print pc"]
+t, clean = session(c)
+for i, k in enumerate(("sw", "sd", "ld", "lw")):
+    v = nth(t, "pc", i)
+    row("p revP %s pc" % k, "absent" if v is None else "0x%x" % v, "0x%x" % RA,
+        "POST: accepted %s CRIME_REV from the window -> ra" % k)
+row("p revP once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "CONTROL: the session refused exactly once (the lb)")
+
+# ---- P2: the twelve-cell value session, POST.  KILLS K1 (INTMASK 4W) and K3 (CONTROL 8R).
+#      The two CONTROL writes at the end drive the clause-(e) 0x0001 and 0x0201 cells into the
+#      `unimplemented' count row, so one count row covers three accesses. ----
+A = Addr(CODE)
+c = POST(A)
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x08, "s0"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x10, "s1"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x18, 0x10000000); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x18, "s2"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x38, 0x11111111); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x38, "s3"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x40, 0); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x40, 0x0a0b0c0d); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x40, "a1"); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x40, "a2"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x08, 0x01); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x08, 0x0001); c += x          # EXTRA: CONTROL 4W 0x0001
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x08, 0x0201); c += x          # EXTRA: CONTROL 4W 0x0201 (clause-(e) cell)
+t, clean = session(c + ["print s0", "print s1", "print s2", "print s3", "print a1", "print a2"])
+tv = nth(t, "s3", 0)
+row("p acc ctl ld", pr(t, "s0"), "0x100", "POST: accepted ld +0x08 answers CONTROL")
+row("p acc istat ld", pr(t, "s1"), "0x%x" % BIT, "POST: accepted ld +0x10 answers INTSTAT")
+row("p acc imask sw", pr(t, "s2"), "0x1000000000000000", "POST: accepted sw +0x18 lands high")
+row("p acc time sd", "absent" if tv is None else ("odd" if tv & 1 else "even"), "odd",
+    "POST: accepted sd +0x38 odd seed stays odd (raw %s)" % tv)
+row("p acc 40 lw", pr(t, "a1"), "0xa0b0c0d", "POST: accepted lw +0x40 answers the stored half")
+row("p acc 40 sw", pr(t, "a2"), "0xa0b0c0d00000000", "POST: accepted sw +0x40 lands high")
+row("p acc ctl msg", count(t, "CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented", clean), 3,
+    "POST: the three accepted CONTROL writes (0x01 sd, 0x0001 sw, 0x0201 sw) each print")
+row("p acc once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "CONTROL: exactly one refusal (the lb)")
+
+# ---- P3: INTSTAT's reassert channel (cause.IP2), POST.  The reassert is an EFFECT channel a
+#      value row cannot see, which is why this session exists separately. ----
+A = Addr(CODE)
+c = POST(A)
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
+c += ["print cause"]
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x10, 0); c += x
+c += ["print cause"]
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, 0); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
+c += ["print cause"]
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x10, 0); c += x
+c += ["print cause"]
+t, clean = session(c)
+row("p istw pre", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 0)), "clear", "CONTROL: IP2 low after SOFTINT default-arm store")
+row("p istw sd", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 1)), "set", "POST: accepted sd +0x10 reasserts")
+row("p istw mid", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 2)), "clear", "CONTROL: lowered again")
+row("p istw sw", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 3)), "set", "POST: accepted sw +0x10 reasserts")
+
+# ---- P4/P5: THE TIME 4-BYTE CELLS, POST, IN THE **GAPPED** SHAPE.  *** THE TWO OBVIOUS SHAPES
+#      WERE MEASURED VACUOUS (post7b.py) and one gave a FALSE RED on a control; only this one
+#      discriminates, at ~13,000x (base 0x13414f8 vs the bypass's 0x5ee). ***  The gap is the
+#      debugger's own pause BETWEEN steps: host time passes, so an update that is performed
+#      arrives before the final 8R and one that is skipped arrives after it.  CHANNEL: a read
+#      takes odata BEFORE its own crime_update_crime_time(). ----
+def gapped_post(kind):
+    ops = [enc("lb", "a3", "t1"),                                            # REFUSED lb -- sets the latch
+           enc("ld", "at", "v1"),                                            # TIME 8R, TIME still ~0
+           enc("sw", "at", "t0") if kind == "w" else enc("lw", "at", "a0"),  # the cell under test
+           enc("ld", "at", "v0")]                                            # what it left behind
+    c = ["at=0x%x" % (CRIME + 0x38), "a3=0x%x" % (CRIME + 0x07), "t0=0x0a0b0c0d",
+         "v0=0x%x" % SENT, "v1=0x%x" % SENT, "a0=0x%x" % SENT, "t1=0x%x" % SENT]
+    for i, op in enumerate(ops):
+        c.append("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, op))
+    c += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
+          "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP), "pc=0x%x" % CODE]
+    c += ["step 2", "step 1", "step 1", "print v0", "print v1"]
+    t, clean = session(c)
+    return nth(t, "v0", 0), nth(t, "v1", 0), (t.count("-- declined.") if clean else "unclean")
+
+
+_gapnote = []
+for kind in ("w", "r"):
+    v0, v1, n = gapped_post(kind)
+    _gapnote.append(n)
+    row("p t4%s gap" % kind, mag(v0), "large",
+        "step2[lb,ld] step1[%s] step1[ld] (raw %s, first ld %s)"
+        % ("sw" if kind == "w" else "lw", None if v0 is None else hex(v0), None if v1 is None else hex(v1)))
+row("p t gap once", _gapnote, [1, 1], "CONTROL: each gapped session announced its one refusal")
+
+# ---- P6: the remaining post-latch ACCEPT cells that carry a value, plus the two EXTRA accesses
+#      (DOG 4W high, the +0x04 write line). ----
+A = Addr(CODE)
+c = POST(A)
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x00, "s0"); c += x                      # REV 8R from CODE
+x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x04, "s1"); c += x                      # REV 4R low lane (devsig cell)
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x04, 0x0c0ffee0); c += x                # EXTRA: the +0x04 WRITE line
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x30, 0x1122334455667788); c += x        # DOG 8W
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x30, "s2"); c += x                      # DOG 8R
+x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x30, "a0"); c += x                      # DOG 4R high
+x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x34, "a1"); c += x                      # DOG 4R low
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x34, 0x0badcafe); c += x                # DOG 4W low
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x30, "s3"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x30, 0x0feedbac); c += x                # EXTRA: DOG 4W HIGH
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x30, "ra"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x                       # INTMASK 8W
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x18, "a2"); c += x                      # INTMASK 8R
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x278, 0x0102030405060708); c += x       # far block 8W
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x278, "t1"); c += x
+t, clean = session(c + ["print s0", "print s1", "print s2", "print a0", "print a1", "print s3",
+                        "print ra", "print a2", "print t1"] + roundtrip(A))
+row("p rev ld", pr(t, "s0"), "0x11", "POST: accepted ld +0x00 answers CRIME_REV")
+row("p rev lw4", pr(t, "s1"), "0x11", "POST: accepted lw +0x04 answers REV low lane")
+row("p dog st+ld", pr(t, "s2"), "0x1122334455667788", "POST: 8W then 8R at CRIME_DOG")
+row("p dog rhi", pr(t, "a0"), "0x11223344", "POST: 4R of the high half (+0x30)")
+row("p dog rlo", pr(t, "a1"), "0x55667788", "POST: 4R of the low half (+0x34)")
+row("p dog wlo", pr(t, "s3"), "0x112233440badcafe", "POST: 4W low half lands, high half kept")
+row("p dog whi", pr(t, "ra"), "0xfeedbac0badcafe", "POST: 4W HIGH half lands, low half kept")
+row("p imask st+ld", pr(t, "a2"), "0x%x" % BIT, "POST: 8W then 8R at CRIME_INTMASK")
+row("p top st+ld", pr(t, "t1"), "0x102030405060708", "POST: 8W then 8R at +0x278")
+row("p resid once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "CONTROL: exactly one refusal (the lb)")
+row("p resid surv", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "CONTROL: the host survives (a RAM trip)")
+
+#  *** REGISTER COLLISION, CAUGHT WHILE DRAFTING AND RECORDED RATHER THAN SILENTLY FIXED: the
+#  prototype's residual-survival row reads `v1', which roundtrip() writes.  The DOG 4W-high
+#  readback added here therefore CANNOT use v1 -- it would make one of the two rows read the
+#  other's register and go green for the wrong reason, which is exactly the vacuity this repair
+#  exists to close.  It is parked in `t2' above.  BEFORE APPLYING: check t2 is not otherwise
+#  live in this session (st() uses a3/t0, ld() uses at; t1 is the far-block readback), and
+#  re-run the negative controls -- a register collision is invisible to a green run.
+
+
 npass = sum(1 for _, ok in rows if ok)
 st_ = dict(rows)
```

## THE FLAGSHIP RULING, PART A (closing pass 6)

## PART A -- #529 crimewidthkill, closing pass 6, repair 11, 141 rows

### R1 -- A1 is a FLOOR breach, L2, and it is a CLASS.  Verified against the source, not taken from the seat.

The width arm (`dev_sgi_ip32.c:275-296`) branches on `len == 8`, `len == 4`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr & 4`; every
accepted access then reaches `switch (relative_addr)` (`:322`) and `d->reg[relative_addr / 8]` (`:277-290`).  So the
LABEL is a floor axis of the width guard under the JOIN clause (the selector flows into an lvalue the guarded body reads
and writes, and the switch re-tests it after the join).  A growth `len == 8 && relative_addr != CRIME_CONTROL` is the
canonical accept-row-failability mutant -- the joint guard-growth form -- and item 6 CLAIMS the accept side is pinned
"at any single cell".  Measured: C1, G1-G5 each 141/141, and G1/G2/G4/G5 change what the guest sees (an 8R of CONTROL
answers 0 not 0x100; an 8R of INTSTAT answers 0 with an interrupt pending; a 4W to REV from the window no longer
redirects; a 4W to INTMASK does not land).  All four of the seat's grounds hold on reading: the de-escalation clause
(pre-fix this edit ended the process at the first such access), the JOIN clause, item 6's own sentence, and the
`#499` shape (grok's `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0` was repaired at +0x00 by `acc8 near` and nowhere else).

**Why it is a class and not six cells:** `rearm once` and `latch x5` COUNT `-- declined.` (probe `:765`, `:949`).  A
wrongly declined accepted cell either comes after the first refusal (silenced) or IS the first refusal (takes the one
notice the lh +0x06 should have had).  Either way the count is 1.  So ACCEPTS carries the latch cross and carries nothing
about acceptance -- for any of its 46 cells.  Six seats read the enumeration and said CLOSES; none of the six engaged
with what the two ACCEPTS-fed rows can OBSERVE.  Counting arguments: one measured clean sweep outweighs seven readings
that did not look at the channel.

### R2 -- codex's row is the class closure for the ANNOUNCED form, and it is general by construction.

`row("latch said", said(t, 2, 0x06, clean), 1, ...)` in latch x5 (`said` at `:381` counts the exact announce text).  All
46 ACCEPTS accesses precede the lh +0x06 in that session (`:934-940`), so any guard growth at any of them sends that cell
into the announcing decline arm (`:314-319`) and the lh's own text is absent.  The window kinds are covered too: the
width arm declines BEFORE the pc test, so `swP/sdP/ldP/lwP` announce like any other (G4 measured red).  Base 149/149, red
on C1 and G1-G5, no shipped row moved, no command list changed.  **Take it as written; do not fold it into `latch x5`**
(a folded row loses the attribution of WHICH half failed).

### RA1 -- the SILENT bypass (G6).  (i) is REQUIRED; (ii) is LICENSED; the cell set is the ENUMERATION's or none.

G6 (`if (len == 8 && relative_addr == CRIME_CONTROL) goto crime_declined;` before the width test) is outside the
de-escalation class -- pre-fix an inserted `return 1;` was equally silent, so the deleted `exit(1)` never announced it --
and outside the DEPENDS-ON enumeration (it inserts a predicate; it weakens none).  So it is NOT floor, and no row can be
DEMANDED for it.  But item 6's literal "any single cell" is false of it, and a false record is L4 whatever the grade.

* **(i) REQUIRED:** narrow item 6 to "the ACCEPT SET shrunk at any single cell IN THE GUARD-GROWTH (announcing) FORM --
  carried by `latch said` over every ACCEPTS cell", and DISCLOSE the silent bypass BY CLASS, naming the cells where a
  value row catches it anyway (below) and the cells where it is EQUIVALENT at the guest (REV 8W/4W/4R out of the window,
  CONTROL 4R: the prelude rewrites or the high half is 0).
  *** THIS CLAUSE ALSO NAMED **TIME 4W/4R** AS EQUIVALENT, AND CLOSING PASS 7 MEASURED IT GUEST-VISIBLE -- mutant E2 at
  157/157, differing on four channels (`regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/opus_close7_20260915/OPUS_REPORT.md', E2).
  The reason the ruling gave is wrong in the same place: a read takes `odata' BEFORE its own crime_update_crime_time(),
  so a skipped update ARRIVES ONE ACCESS LATE instead of accumulating.  Corrected here rather than only in the probe
  because item 6's wrong sentence was COPIED FROM THIS ONE, and correcting one of the two leaves the record
  inconsistent -- the seat that measured it said so explicitly.  The ruling's own disposition is otherwise untouched:
  TIME 4W/4R is now an unrowed single-axis FLOOR cell owed by repair 13. ***
  *** RA3 of this ruling -- "It is clause (e) by the DEPENDS-ON bound (`d->warned' is not a field the accept bodies
  read)" -- IS **OVERTURNED TO FLOOR** BY THE 2026-09-16 ADJUDICATION (FABLE_C5_RULING_20260915.md, S-A.7), ON FOUR
  INDEPENDENT GROUNDS, THE FIRST OF WHICH IS IN THE DIFF ITSELF: the fix's own decline arm READS `d->warned' at
  dev_sgi_ip32.c:314, in a hunk THIS DIFF ADDS, so the latch is a device-state lvalue the fix's own repair path reads --
  a floor axis by the pass-11 floor definition and the M_ICLR precedent, and by this round's own practice, since it
  built and measured the shrink.  Bounded, and not #392: the floor is the diff-closed 56-cell product, of which the
  ROWS OWED are the 23 open guest-visible cells.  *The measuring seat disputed this clause and was right; the ruling
  that adopted it was mine, and a ruling in one's own favour is the one least likely to be tested.* ***
* **(ii) LICENSED (measured delta: `dg ld ctl`, `dg ld istat`, `dg sw imask`, `dg swP pc` each red on its cell, green on
  base, new sessions only).**  If value rows are added -- and I RECOMMEND it, because at those cells the silent form has
  the SAME guest effect as the growth form and a detector that sees one and not the other is claiming more than it
  pins -- the set is the Opus per-cell table's guest-visible "no" cells, all of them, in ONE diff: REV window 4W/8W/8R
  (the redirect), CONTROL 8R/8W, INTSTAT 8W/8R/4W, INTMASK 4W, TIME 8W, default `sw/lw 0x40`.  Twelve cells, each with
  a CHANNEL line in `PRETABLE.out` naming the mutant and the observable.  A five-cell set drawn from the mutants the
  seat happened to build is licensed too, but then the record says "5 of 12 rowed, 7 disclosed" -- it may not say the
  silent class is closed.  What I reject is only the half-set presented as closure.
* The coordinator's worry about incoherence is answered: (i) states what the CONTRACT claims; (ii) states what the
  DETECTOR additionally pins.  Different questions, both answered, no contradiction.

### RA2 -- repair 12 owes TARGETED TABLE 10 and CLOSING PASS 7.  The seat's augmentation run does not discharge either.

* **Table 10, and why the run is not it:** the seat APPENDED rows to a frozen copy; repair 12 re-types them into the
  shipped probe.  A re-typed row is a new instrument and is measured, not assumed.  Legs: base; C1, G1-G6, C2 (the new
  rows' own kills, including G6 for any value rows); the 17 table-9 pre-set kills IF POSTTABLE 10 will state their new
  scores (the "they also red `latch said`" prediction is REASONED -- the seat says so -- and a predicted score in a
  measured column is the frozen-number class; run them or omit them from the record, ~17 legs, about an hour in four
  lanes).  The 12 re-arm kills and every kill outside the two sessions cannot move: no command list changed (the
  targeting rule), and I checked that `latch said` reads latch x5's existing transcript only.
* **Pass 7 is owed** because the first VALID pass with zero floor breaches surviving adjudication is not pass 6.  It is
  LIGHT and it VERIFIES rather than hunts: (a) for each of the 46 ACCEPTS cells, name the row that reds under an
  ANNOUNCED decline (`latch said`, by construction) and under a SILENT decline (a value row, or "equivalent", or
  "disclosed") -- 92 cells, derivable by reading; (b) confirm by reading latch x5's command list that every ACCEPTS
  access precedes the lh (the generality argument); (c) nothing else.  Full roster, per the every-stage rule.

### RA3 -- is there a further accept-side class?  Yes, and it is the one pass 7 enumerates; plus one thing to BUILD.

* The accept side of the 46-cell cross has exactly two forms a single edit can produce: ANNOUNCED decline (closed by
  `latch said`) and SILENT decline (RA1).  That is the enumeration pass 7 verifies: cell x {announced, silent}.
* **A `d->warned`-keyed shrink must be BUILT before it is disclosed.**  The seat reasoned it clause (e) and did not build
  it.  Two forms: keyed on 8R (`len == 8 && (writeflag == MEM_WRITE || !(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))`) -- I predict
  KILLED: `sb keeps`/`sh keeps`/`lo keeps`/`hi keeps`/`mid keeps` (`:691-842`) each read a seeded slot with an accepted ld
  AFTER a refusal, which such a shrink answers 0; and keyed on 8W -- may escape (the only accepted 8W after a refusal
  whose value is read back by an accepted read is unclear from the rows).  It is clause (e) by the DEPENDS-ON bound
  (`d->warned` is not a field the accept bodies read); if it escapes, DISCLOSE it by name.  "Reasoned, not built" is a
  prediction and this chain has had enough of those.
* Classes NOT to reopen: accepted-but-mishandled (landing, lane, re-map) are items 6/7's anchor rows and earlier tables'
  material, outside this chain's class.  Pass 7 is told so explicitly, or the chain does not terminate.

### L4 wrong records in Part A (file: `regress/crime_width_probe.py` unless noted)

   `:495` "ENDIANESS (0x0100) is the arm's third tested bit"** -- FALSE at both sites: `:375` is `idata &=
   ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;` with no `if`.  The header's own repair-11 note (`:728`) says the opposite correctly.  Two
   sites; the grep-for-siblings rule.
3. **DISCLOSED "ACCEPT cells at unrowed slots"** -- implies a touched slot is pinned; touching pins the LATCH, never the
   acceptance.
4. **DISCLOSED "an accepted WRITE at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away ... no round trip there can observe"** -- wrong twice: the
   window 4W redirects (G4 measured), and the 8-byte CONTROL write is unrowed (G1).
5. **Repair-11 comment (`:725-726`) "crossed the window with the DIRECTION on writes only"** -- the missing cell was the
   8-byte WIDTH on the READ side; say that.
6. **"the two label cells whose arms have NO body"** -- three: REV from OUTSIDE the window is a label cell with no body
   for that access.  Name it.
7. **`killtable9_20260914/POSTTABLE.out` FLOOR line** -- true for pre-set/re-arm; add the note "the ACCEPT side of the
   same cross is NOT closed by ACCEPTS (closing pass 6)" in the style it already uses.
8. **`panel_20260915_011138/brief_sent.md` "mutants of table 9, verbatim"** shows P3-P7; table 9 ran P1/P1b/P2/P2b only
   (`mutants529i.py` imports exactly those; codex and agy both caught it).  A frozen artifact; the ledger entry for this
   panel carries the correction.
9. **The adjudication brief itself:** "Six seats CLOSE (...)" lists SEVEN names.  Not a shipped record; noted so the
   ledger entry does not copy it.
10. `free_one` ignoring `clean` (codex): a truncated transcript can only produce a false RED there (count 0 against want
    1), never a false green -- not a vacuity, but fix it for consistency with `said`.

## THE MUTANTS OF TABLE 10, verbatim (table 9's by name; the Opus close-6 seat's C1/C2/G1-G6; this table's own)

```python
M = dict(J.M)

LEN8 = "\tif (len == 8) {\n"
LEN4 = "\t} else if (len == 4) {\n"
W, R_ = "writeflag == MEM_WRITE", "writeflag == MEM_READ"

#  ---- the three the close-7 seat MEASURED, verbatim (each 157/157 against repair 12) ----------------------------
M.update({
    "K1": [(LEN4, "\t} else if (len == 4 && !(%s &&\n\t    relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK && (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))) {\n" % W)],
    "K2": [(LEN4, "\t} else if (len == 4 && !(relative_addr == CRIME_REV && (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))) {\n")],
    "K3": [(LEN8, "\tif (len == 8 && !(%s &&\n\t    relative_addr == CRIME_CONTROL && (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))) {\n" % R_)],
    #  codex's TIME mutant, verbatim: the cell item 6 and the ruling's RA1 both called EQUIVALENT and which is
    #  measured guest-visible on four channels.  Killed only by the GAPPED row shape.
    "E2": J.bypass("len == 4 && relative_addr == CRIME_TIME"),
})

#  ---- THE CLOSURE, GENERATED.  28 latch-set cells; the generator IS the enumeration. ---------------------------
LABELS = (("rev", "relative_addr == CRIME_REV"),
          ("ctl", "relative_addr == CRIME_CONTROL"),
          ("ist", "relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT"),
          ("imk", "relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK"),
          ("dog", "relative_addr == CRIME_DOG"),
          ("tim", "relative_addr == CRIME_TIME"),
          #  the `default' arm is the COMPLEMENT of the six, and it is written as one so the mutant cannot be
          #  satisfied by a label the switch already names -- the enumeration's own negative space.
          ("dfl", "relative_addr != CRIME_REV && relative_addr != CRIME_CONTROL &&\n\t    "
                  "relative_addr != CRIME_INTSTAT && relative_addr != CRIME_INTMASK &&\n\t    "
                  "relative_addr != CRIME_DOG && relative_addr != CRIME_TIME"))


def klatch(anchor, head, dir_expr, label_expr):
    """the K-form: keep the width guard, null its repair at ONE cell ONCE THE LATCH HAS FIRED (silent by construction,
    because the announce is what the latch suppresses)"""
    return [(anchor, "%s && !(%s && (%s) &&\n\t    (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))) {\n" % (head, dir_expr, label_expr))]


for lab, expr in LABELS:
    for ln, anchor, head in ((8, LEN8, "\tif (len == 8"), (4, LEN4, "\t} else if (len == 4")):
        for d, dex in (("w", W), ("r", R_)):
            name = "C_%s%d%s" % (lab, ln, d)
            if name not in M:                       # K1/K2/K3 are members of this product; do not shadow them
                M[name] = klatch(anchor, head, dex, expr)

#  K2 is the (rev, 4, both-directions) cell -- deliberately NOT direction-split, because that is the form the seat
#  MEASURED.  The generated C_rev4w / C_rev4r are its two halves and are kept: a cell whose two halves are covered
#  separately is not thereby covered jointly, and this table is the one that says so.

```

## MUTATION-TEST TABLE 10 -- results (`POSTTABLE.out`)

```
POSTTABLE 11 -- #529 crimewidthkill, repair 13 (188 rows).  Written AFTER the table; PRETABLE.out predates it and the
mtimes prove the order (PRETABLE 15:48:34, mutator 15:48:43, table launched 15:49:51).

RESULT   33 legs, every one ending CRIMEP_DONE with done=1.  Driver token KILL529K_DONE present.  table11.out.
         BASE 188/188 CONTROL=OK.  Clone RESTORED and verified before the probe phase even began: cmp-clean against
         GXEMUL-SEC, binary newer than source, sentinel cleared, and `restored == base (byte-identical)'.

THE GAP CLOSING PASS 7 FOUND IS CLOSED, AND IT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED
         K1 187/188  reds `p acc imask sw'                      (was 157/157 -- an ESCAPE -- before repair 13)
         K2 186/188  reds `p revP sw pc', `p revP lw pc'        (was 157/157)
         K3 187/188  reds `p acc ctl ld'                        (was 157/157)
         E2 186/188  reds `p t4w gap', `p t4r gap'              (was 157/157; the TIME cell item 6 and the prior
                     ruling's RA1 both called EQUIVALENT, measured guest-visible on four channels)

THE ENUMERATION VERIFIES: 28 of 28 GENERATED CLOSURE CELLS BEHAVED AS THE CONTRACT SAYS THEY MUST
         27 cells KILLED, each reddening rows at its own (len, direction, label) cell.
          1 cell at 188/188 -- `C_ctl4r' -- AND THAT IS THE CONTRACT'S OWN NEGATIVE CONTROL PASSING, NOT AN ESCAPE.
            The contract lists CONTROL 4R among the EQUIVALENT cells ("reads 0 either way"), so a latch-keyed shrink
            there has nothing to observe and MUST move nothing.  It moved nothing.  *** This is the enumeration
            checking itself: the same generator emitted the cells that must red and the cell that must not, and both
            came out as written.  A closure that only produced kills would not have tested its own EQUIVALENT claim
            at all. ***

*** MY PER-ROW PREDICTION FOR K2 WAS WRONG, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS RIGHT.  PRETABLE 11 predicted K2 would red "the
FOUR `p revP * pc' rows"; it reds TWO -- `sw' and `lw'.  K2 is `} else if (len == 4 && !(...))', on the len == 4 ARM,
so it can only reach the 4-BYTE ops; `sd' and `ld' are 8-byte, go through the len == 8 arm, and cannot be affected by
it.  I wrote the prediction from the CELL (REV) without carrying the WIDTH the mutant is gated on. ***
         K1, K3 and E2 matched their per-row predictions EXACTLY.
         This is the predict-then-measure discipline earning its keep in the direction it is meant to: the error was
         in the prediction, the detector and the mutant are both correct, and nothing was fitted to the answer.

THREE LEGS CARRY `CRIMEP_CONTROL=FAIL', AND IT IS INFORMATIONAL RATHER THAN A DEFECT -- but it is a finding about the
INSTRUMENT and is written down rather than waved past.
         C_ist4r 187/188  the ONLY red is `istat ctl'
         C_imk4r 187/188  the ONLY red is `imask ctl'
         C_tim8r 181/188  reds `time lb/sb/lh/sh' and the new `p acc', `p t4w', `p t4r'
         In each case the red rows ARE the cell's own channel, so every kill is ATTRIBUTABLE -- the mutant failed for
         the reason under test.  What the aggregate cannot do is tell "the mutant broke a control row" apart from
         "this session was invalid", because `CRIMEP_CONTROL' is a conjunction over a fixed row set and some of those
         rows are the very channels a latch-keyed shrink at INTSTAT/INTMASK/TIME is supposed to move.
         *** THE GATE IS UNAFFECTED: it pins CONTROL=OK on the BASE build, and base is 188/188 CONTROL=OK.  The FAIL
         appears only under mutants, where a moved control row is the expected outcome. ***
         FILED, not fixed: the control aggregate could name WHICH control rows moved instead of collapsing to a
         boolean.  That is an instrument change owing its own negative controls, and this round takes one site.

NOT RE-MEASURED, with the reason: every table 1-10 kill.  Repair 13 added NEW SESSIONS ONLY -- no existing session's
         command list changed -- so by the targeting rule an adds-only repair moves no kill and none is re-run.

RECORDS   the three gate pins moved 157 -> 188 with the precommit comment; the score-claims checker then reported NINE
         stale claims in the new contract text and was RIGHT -- seven were historical quotes needing an era tag ("on
         the 157-row test"), and TWO were FALSE score-claims (`0xc6/0x108' and `6/6 each way' parse as N/M), which
         were REPHRASED rather than tagged, because an era tag on a hex value would be a lie told to quiet a checker.
         SCORE_CLAIMS_PASS now.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT SETTLE: closing pass 8 still has to run, and by the repair-chain amendment its job is to VERIFY
         THE ENUMERATION rather than hunt cells -- does a clean-sweep latch-SET mutant exist in the 56-cell product
         that this table did not build?  It must be a VALID pass and must close on a zero carried WITH its
         denominator (which cells, which shapes were swept), never a bare zero.
```

## QUESTIONS -- *** THIS PASS VERIFIES AN ENUMERATION.  IT DOES NOT HUNT CELLS. ***

The repair-chain amendment governs this pass, and the flagship ruling repeats it: when two consecutive passes find
breaches of ONE class, the next repair owes the CLASS CLOSURE in one diff, and **the closing pass then VERIFIES THE
ENUMERATION rather than hunting**.  Three passes found this class (pass 6 the announced accept-guard growth, table 10
the width-level latch shrink, pass 7 the per-cell latch shrink); repair 13 is the closure; table 11 measured it.

**A BARE ZERO DOES NOT CLOSE THE CHAIN.**  If your answer is "no gap", it must carry its DENOMINATOR -- which cells,
which mutant shapes you swept -- because "zero found" and "zero looked for" must not render the same.

1. **IS THE ENUMERATION ITSELF RIGHT?**  It is `{latch clear, set} x {len 4, 8} x {read, write} x {the SEVEN labels the
   switch distinguishes: REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG, TIME, default}` = 56 cells, read off `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`
   and generated mechanically (the `klatch`/`LABELS` block above emits all 28 latch-SET cells by construction).
   **Is there an AXIS it is missing**, or a label the switch distinguishes that the seven do not cover?  Check it
   against the function, not against my description of the function.
2. **DOES A CLEAN-SWEEP LATCH-SET MUTANT EXIST THAT TABLE 11 DID NOT BUILD?**  Table 11 built 28 generated cells plus
   K1/K2/K3 and E2: 27 killed, and `C_ctl4r` scored 188/188 because the contract calls CONTROL 4R EQUIVALENT ("reads 0
   either way") -- that is the enumeration's own negative control passing.  Give an exact edit to
   `src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c` that scores **188/188**, and name the row you expected to catch it.
3. **IS THE PER-CELL DISPOSITION HONEST?**  The contract splits the 46 ACCEPTS entries as **23 ROWED / 8 ALREADY PINNED
   post-latch / 10 EQUIVALENT / 5 HOST-DIAGNOSTIC (clause-(e), disclosed)**.  The 8 come from a STATIC session map
   (`opus_close7_20260915/scripts2/session_map2.py`), not from a measurement.  **Is any cell in the wrong bucket?**  A
   cell called EQUIVALENT that a guest can observe is a floor breach; a cell called ROWED whose row cannot vary under
   its own mutant is vacuous.
4. **THREE LEGS REPORT `CRIMEP_CONTROL=FAIL`** (`C_ist4r`, `C_imk4r`, `C_tim8r`).  In each the red rows are that cell's
   own channel, so I read every kill as ATTRIBUTABLE and the gate as unaffected (it pins CONTROL=OK on BASE, which is
   188/188 OK).  **Is that reading wrong** -- is any of those three kills actually an invalid session rather than a
   caught mutant?
5. **WHICH STATEMENT IN THIS BRIEF, IN `POSTTABLE 11`, OR IN THE PROBE HEADER IS WRONG?**  One is already known and is
   recorded rather than hidden: PRETABLE 11 predicted K2 would red FOUR `p revP * pc` rows and it reds TWO, because K2
   sits on the `len == 4` arm and the 8-byte ops cannot reach it.  That was a prediction error, not a detector defect.
   Find the next one.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

  23: 
  24: ## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)
  25: 
  26: * **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of eight passes found it correct): a refused width is announced once per
  27:   device instance (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common
  28:   tail, which restores HARDINT, recomputes INTSTAT (it does NOT call crime_interrupt_reassert, so the line is untouched),
  29:   answers a read 0 and returns 1.
  30: * **History**: 40 -> ... -> 141 -> 157 rows; TEN mutation-test tables, each with a written PRETABLE and a mechanical
  31:   prediction checker.  (The sixth-pass brief called the Opus fifth-closing seat's mutants "P1-P7"; table 9 ran four.)
  32: * **ACCEPTS** = 46 ENTRIES: the 40-cell pre-set/re-arm floor + 2 ENDIANESS strengthenings + 4 positional repeats.
  33: * **Free-running vs stepped**: a stepped probe cannot tell debug() from fatal(); a free-running one can (verbose == 0).
  34: * **TIME** updates add 66 x elapsed microseconds -- always EVEN -- so an odd seed stays odd.
  35: 
  36: ## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)
  37: 
  38: ```c
  39: DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
  40: {
  41: 	/*
  42: 	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
  43: 	 *  physical 0x14000000.
  44: 	 *
  45: 	 *  On my real O2:
  46: 	 *
  47: 	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  48: 	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  49: 	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  50: 	 *
  51: 	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
  52: 	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
  53: 	 *  would work too.)
  54: 	 *
  55: 	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
  56: 	 */
  57: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
  58: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  59: 	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
  60: 	size_t i;
  61: 
  62: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  63: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  64: 
  65: 	/*
  66: 	 *  Set crime version/revision:
  67: 	 *
  68: 	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
  69: 	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
  70: 	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
  71: 	 *
  72: 	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
  73: 	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
  74: 	 */
  75: 	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;
  76: 
  77: 	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
  78: 
  79: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
  80: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
  81: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
  82: 
  83: 	/*
  84: 	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
  85: 	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
  86: 	 *
  87: 	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
  88: 	 *  bank #0."
  89: 	 */
  90: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
  91: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
  92: 		exit(1);
  93: 	}
  94: 
  95: 	int mb_per_bank;
  96: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
  97: 		mb_per_bank = 32;
  98: 	else
  99: 		mb_per_bank = 128;
 100: 
 101: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
 102: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
 103: 			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
 104: 			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
 105: 		exit(1);
 106: 	}
 107: 
 108: 	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
 109: 	size_t total_mb = 0;
 110: 	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
 111: 		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
 112: 		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
 113: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
 114: 				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
 115: 		else
 116: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;
 117: 
 118: 		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
 119: 	}
 120: 	
 121: 	if (len == 8) {
 122: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 123: 			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
 124: 		else
 125: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 126: 	} else if (len == 4) {
 127: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 128: 			if (relative_addr & 4) {
 129: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
 130: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
 131: 			} else {
 132: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
 133: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
 134: 			}
 135: 		} else {
 136: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 137: 			if (relative_addr & 4)
 138: 				odata = (int32_t)odata;
 139: 			else
 140: 				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
 141: 		}
 142: 	} else {
 143: 		/*
 144: 		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
 145: 		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
 146: 		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
 147: 		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
 148: 		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
 149: 		 *
 150: 		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
 151: 		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
 152: 		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
 153: 		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
 154: 		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
 155: 		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
 156: 		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
 157: 		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
 158: 		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
 159: 		 */
 160: 		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
 161: 			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
 162: 			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
 163: 			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
 164: 		}
 165: 		goto crime_declined;
 166: 	}
 167: 
 168: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 169: 
 170: 	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
 171: 		/*
 172: 		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
 173: 		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
 174: 		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
 175: 		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
 176: 		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
 177: 		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
 178: 		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
 179: 		 *
 180: 		 *  The address where this happens is:
 181: 		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
 182: 		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
 183: 		 *
 184: 		 *  4.18 works too.
 185: 		 *
 186: 		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
 187: 		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
 188: 		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
 189: 		 */
 190: 		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
 191: 			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
 192: 			if (cpu->is_32bit)
 193: 				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 194: 			else
 195: 				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 196: 		}
 197: 		break;
 198: 
 199: 	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
 200: 		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
 201: 		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
 202: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 203: 			/*
 204: 			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
 205: 			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
 206: 			 */
 207: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
 208: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
 209: 			}
 210: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
 211: 				int j;
 212: 
 213: 				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
 214: 				    "reboot" command:  */
 215: 				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
 216: 					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
 217: 
 218: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
 219: 			}
 220: 
 221: 			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
 222: 
 223: 			if (idata != 0)
 224: 				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
 225: 				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 226: 		}
 227: 		break;
 228: 
 229: 	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
 230: 	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
 231: 		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 232: 		break;
 233: 
 234: 	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
 235: 		// No warning.
 236: 		break;
 237: 
 238: 	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
 239: 		crime_update_crime_time(d);
 240: 		break;
 241: 
 242: 	default:
 243: 		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
 244: 			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
 245: 			    (int)relative_addr, len);
 246: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 247: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 248: 			debug(" ]\n");
 249: 		} else {
 250: 			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
 251: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 252: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 253: 			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
 254: 		}
 255: 	}
 256: 
 257:  crime_declined:
 258: 	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
 259: 	    and is still answered.  */
 260: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
 261: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 262: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 263: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 264: 
 265: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 266: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 267: 
 268: 	return 1;
 269: }
 270: ```
 271:

 274: ```
 275: CONTRACT -- the edits this detector is designed to catch (clause (e)), each MEASURED in a kill table under

 278:                                                        <op> surv (six widths), hi surv, latch surv, sb08 run

 280:   3. the refused access FALLING INTO THE REGISTER SWITCH -- the goto lost, or narrowed by width, direction, or ADDRESS.
 281:      The switch's observable arms, per refusal cell:
 282:         CRIME_CONTROL (+0x08), writes     sb 0x01 prints the unimplemented-control fatal    sb08 msg

 succeeded in 208ms:
 301:                                           at +0x07, +0x06, +0x1f, +0x1e, +0x27f  hi nodflt, and

 310:      observable label carries the whole {1,2} x {R,W} refusal floor (CONTROL's reads and DOG have no body: below).
 311:      *** THE sh08 AND sw08 CHANNELS EXIST BECAUSE OF A SEPARATE DEFECT, `crimectllane' (filed): the switch matches
 312:      CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at

 314:      removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***

 455: *** THE LATCH-KEYED ACCEPT SHRINK (the K-CLASS) AND ITS CLOSURE -- repair 13, and it is the reason this probe grew
 456: from 157 rows to 188. ***
 457: 
 458: An accept guard grown with a conjunct on THIS FIX'S OWN LATCH -- `&& !(<cell> && (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))' -- declines
 459: SILENTLY at that cell once any refusal has happened, because the latch is exactly what suppresses the announce.  Three
 460: instances were MEASURED at 157/157 with a guest loss on the 157-row instrument (closing pass 7, opus_close7_20260915/
 461: OPUS_REPORT.md): K1 at INTMASK 4W, K2 at the REV 4-byte cell, K3 at CONTROL 8R.  Every value row this probe had was
 462: measured with the latch CLEAR, so none of them could see it.
 463: 
 464: *** THE GRADE IS FLOOR, NOT CLAUSE (e), and that OVERTURNED THE PRIOR RULING (FABLE_C5_RULING_20260915.md, S-A.7;
 465: FABLE_C4_RULING's RA3 corrected in place).  The reason is in the fix's own diff: the width dispatch's `else' arm READS
 466: `d->warned', in a hunk this diff ADDS -- so the latch is a device-state lvalue the fix's own repair path reads, which
 467: the pass-11 floor definition and the M_ICLR precedent make a floor axis. ***
 468: 
 469: THE ENUMERATION is the four-axis product {latch clear, set} x {len 4, 8} x {read, write} x {the 7 labels the switch
 470: distinguishes: REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG, TIME, default} = 56 cells.  The latch-CLEAR half is table 10's
 471: twelve S_* silent bypasses.  **THE ROWS OWED ARE NOT ALL 28 LATCH-SET MUTANTS AND NOT ALL 56 CELLS**: a mutant at an
 472: equivalent or already-pinned cell teaches nothing and a row at a cell no mutant reaches is unfalsifiable, so the FLOOR
 473: is the INTERSECTION.  Over the 46 ACCEPTS entries:
 474: 
 475:     23  ROWED HERE, post-latch, by the `p *' sessions below (the REV window, the value session, the INTSTAT reassert
 476:         channel, the TIME pair, and the residual ACCEPT cells).  Every one runs AFTER a refused lb at +0x07, so the
 477:         latch is SET while the row is measured -- which is the whole point.
 478:      8  ALREADY PINNED post-latch by an incidental access in an existing session.  Derived, not asserted:
 479:         opus_close7_20260915/scripts2/session_map2.py (static, no emulator) maps all 45 sessions and finds 72 accepted
 480:         accesses after their session's first refusal, in 11 sessions, 10 carrying an oracle.
 481:     10  EQUIVALENT at the guest -- nothing to observe, so no row is owed or possible.
 482:      5  HOST-DIAGNOSTIC ONLY: clause-(e) debt, DISCLOSED with its measurements rather than rowed -- CONTROL 0x0201 at
 483:         both widths (F1/F1b, 157/157 on the 157-row test: the `unimplemented' count drops to one, `CTL after ld' is 0x100 either way, and
 484:         the guard is a MULTI-AXIS idata joint, which the 2026-09-04 pass-11 amendment puts in debt) and the `sw +0x04'
 485:         write line (F3, 157/157 on the 157-row test: guest-equivalent; `dflt ctl' counts READS at +0x04, none counts writes).
 486:     --
 487:     46  = the ACCEPTS entry count (46 ENTRIES, six KINDS).
 488: 
 489: *** THE TIME 4W/4R PAIR IS A FLOOR CELL AND ITS ROWS NEEDED A PARTICULAR SHAPE, which is this repair's own evidence
 490: that a CHANNEL line must be RUN against its mutant and not merely written.  The seat that proposed the rows measured
 491: its own first two shapes VACUOUS, and one gave a FALSE RED on a control.  Only the GAPPED form discriminates: the
 492: refused lb and the first 8R in one `step 2', the 4-byte cell in its own `step 1', the final 8R in another -- the
 493: debugger's pause BETWEEN steps is the gap, so an update that happens lands before the final read and one that is
 494: skipped arrives after it.  ~13,000x separation (base 0x13414f8 against the bypass's 0x5ee), reproduced here. ***
 495: 
 496: DISCLOSED, not rowed:
 497:   * `goto crime_declined' -> `return 1' is EQUIVALENT on every channel: memory_rw pre-zeroes a device read buffer
 498:     (#95, src/cpus/memory_rw.c -- `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)' then `memset(data, 0, len);', immediately above
 499:     `paddr -= mem->devices[i].baseaddr;'), and on a declined access nothing between the entry and the tail writes
 500:     HARDINT or SOFTINT, so the tail's restore and recompute are identities.  Measured 40/40 (the then 40-row test) in kill table 1.
 501:   * a fall-through confined to the THREE label cells whose arms have NO body for that access, all EQUIVALENT:
 502:       CRIME_DOG (0x30)          `No warning.' -- the arm is `break;'
 503:       CRIME_CONTROL, reads      the arm acts only on MEM_WRITE
 504:       CRIME_REV, outside the PROM-probe window   the arm acts only when the pc is in the window
 505:     The first two measured as survivors in kill table 2 (`!= CRIME_DOG', the CONTROL-read-only fall-through); the REV
 506:     cell is the `lb/lh rev pc' rows' converse, read from the arm.  [This said "the two label cells" until repair 12 --
 507:     kimi CLI, closing pass 6.]
 508:   * ACCEPT cells at unrowed slots: the rows touch accepted accesses at +0x00, +0x04, +0x08, +0x10, +0x18, +0x30,
 509:     +0x34, +0x38, +0x40, +0x210, +0x214, +0x1f8 and +0x278 (not an exhaustive list of what the window accepts); an
 510:     accept arm narrowed by an address strictly INSIDE the window at an unrowed slot (e.g. `len == 8 && relative_addr !=
 511:     0x100', kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.  TOUCHING a slot in ACCEPTS pins its LATCH
 512:     behaviour, never its acceptance -- what pins acceptance is item 6 (`latch said' for the announced form, the value
 513:     rows for the silent form).  [This bullet implied a touched slot was pinned until repair 12 -- the Opus seat, closing
 514:     pass 6.]
 515:   * an effect ADDED to the decline arm and keyed on ONE UNNAMED offset (item 9 rows the READ answer at every named
 516:     label) -- a refused store that lands only at
 517:     +0x31, a declined read answering nonzero only at +0x20, a silent decline only at +0x100, a fall-through only at
 518:     +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
 519:     offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
 520:     keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
 521:     kind that can carry it (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; an aligned 2-byte access never sets
 522:     bit 0; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8 carried by no refusal at all; codex and the Opus seat,
 523:     fourth closing pass, found 2W LANDING at bit 1 set carried by no read-back store -- now +0x42, `lo keeps').  Note
 524:     `relative_addr / 8': a refused halfword at +0x1e would land on INTMASK's slot, not at +0x1e; the 2W session's
 525:     +0x1e store is not read back, and the INTMASK landing is carried by `imask keeps'.  A landing at
 526:     CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20, not a switch label) is in this class (deepseek, third closing pass).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
 527:     OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
 528:     which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
 529:     (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
 530:   * a ROUND TRIP cannot observe an accepted WRITE at +0x00 or +0x08 (the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL
 531:     on every access) -- but the write's EFFECTS can, and are rowed: from the PROM-probe window an accepted write
 532:     REDIRECTS the pc (revP sw pc, revP sd pc), and at CONTROL the arm's effects (sw08 halts, sw08 msg ctl at 4 bytes;
 533:     acc ctl sd at 8).  [This bullet said "no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted" as though
 534:     nothing could, and it was wrong twice: the window redirect (measured by the Opus seat, G4) and the 8-byte CONTROL
 535:     write, which no row carried until repair 12 -- closing pass 6.]  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc
 536:     ctl' (its redirect needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
 537:   * `odata = idata' in the decline arm: EQUIVALENT by construction -- `idata' is set only under MEM_WRITE and `odata' is
 538:     consumed only under MEM_READ (measured 141/141 on the 141-row test by the Opus seat, closing pass 6).  `cpu->is_32bit' (the REV arm's
 539:     pc-pointer refresh) holds one value per machine, like the configuration lvalues above.  Accepted-side address bits 7
 540:     and 8: the mid session's accepted sd +0x1f8 carries both, before and between its refusals (a latch keyed on them is
 541:     killed -- kimi-k3's C3, 139/141 on the 141-row test); an ACCEPT arm keyed on them is the unrowed-slot class above.
 542:   * ACCEPTS has 46 ENTRIES, not 46 kinds: the 40-cell pre-set/re-arm floor, the two ENDIANESS strengthenings, and four
 543:     positional repeats of default-arm kinds (`sw/lw 0x04' repeat 0x34's low lane; `sd/ld 0x278' repeat 0x40's 8-byte
 544:     kinds, and are the prologue's only accepted access with address bit 9 set -- an unrecorded strengthening until
 545:     repair 12; kimi CLI, closing pass 6).
 546:   * a decline answering a selection of the register's bytes at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME CONFINED to
 547:     bytes the model holds at 0 there -- REV = 0x11: bytes 1-7; CONTROL = 0x100: byte 0 and bytes 2-7; TIME: the 4-byte
 548:     half above the low word -- is EQUIVALENT (the prelude rewrites REV and CONTROL on every access; TIME is kept to 32
 549:     bits).  [Corrected 2026-09-14 from closing pass 5 (glm): this read "the ADDRESSED lanes (bytes 0-1 at a label)",
 550:     which at REV and CONTROL include a NONZERO byte -- item 9's v0 rows exist to catch exactly that selection.]  At
 551:     INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG every byte is seeded nonzero, so any selection shows (Opus, second closing pass; glm, third).
 552:   * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
 553:     default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
 554:   * (closing pass 5; the Opus seat measured each at 141/141 on the 141-row test; the flagship ruling graded each clause
 555:     (e)) a latch re-armed or pre-set by a CONTROL write's ENDIANESS bit (0x100) -- the arm clears it unconditionally and
 556:     never branches on it; the two ENDIANESS writes in ACCEPTS since repair 11 are a strengthening, not a floor claim
 557:     over value bits; the announce CHANNEL keyed on a JOINT of axes (e.g. `(relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT && len == 2 ?
 558:     debug : fatal)(') -- item 5 rows the channel on each SINGLE axis; and a decline answering a MACHINE-CONFIGURATION
 559:     lvalue (`d->use_fb', `flag_for_128MB'), which holds one value per machine and is 0 on this probe's -M 64 non-graphics
 560:     ip32 -- a graphics O2 (use_fb = 1) would see `odata = d->use_fb' answer 1.  Unrowable on one machine, like the
 561:     function-static latch below.
 562:   * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
 563:     CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
 564:   * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
 565:     (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
 566:   * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
 567:     process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
 568:     recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
 569:   * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
 570:     not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
 571:   * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).

 641: +*** THE LATCH-KEYED ACCEPT SHRINK (the K-CLASS) AND ITS CLOSURE -- repair 13, and it is the reason this probe grew
 642: +from 157 rows to 188. ***
 643: +
 644: +An accept guard grown with a conjunct on THIS FIX'S OWN LATCH -- `&& !(<cell> && (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))' -- declines
 645: +SILENTLY at that cell once any refusal has happened, because the latch is exactly what suppresses the announce.  Three
 646: +instances were MEASURED at 157/157 with a guest loss on the 157-row instrument (closing pass 7, opus_close7_20260915/
 647: +OPUS_REPORT.md): K1 at INTMASK 4W, K2 at the REV 4-byte cell, K3 at CONTROL 8R.  Every value row this probe had was
 648: +measured with the latch CLEAR, so none of them could see it.
 649: +
 650: +*** THE GRADE IS FLOOR, NOT CLAUSE (e), and that OVERTURNED THE PRIOR RULING (FABLE_C5_RULING_20260915.md, S-A.7;
 651: +FABLE_C4_RULING's RA3 corrected in place).  The reason is in the fix's own diff: the width dispatch's `else' arm READS
 652: +`d->warned', in a hunk this diff ADDS -- so the latch is a device-state lvalue the fix's own repair path reads, which
 653: +the pass-11 floor definition and the M_ICLR precedent make a floor axis. ***
 654: +
 655: +THE ENUMERATION is the four-axis product {latch clear, set} x {len 4, 8} x {read, write} x {the 7 labels the switch
 656: +distinguishes: REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG, TIME, default} = 56 cells.  The latch-CLEAR half is table 10's
 657: +twelve S_* silent bypasses.  **THE ROWS OWED ARE NOT ALL 28 LATCH-SET MUTANTS AND NOT ALL 56 CELLS**: a mutant at an
 658: +equivalent or already-pinned cell teaches nothing and a row at a cell no mutant reaches is unfalsifiable, so the FLOOR
 659: +is the INTERSECTION.  Over the 46 ACCEPTS entries:
 660: +
 661: +    23  ROWED HERE, post-latch, by the `p *' sessions below (the REV window, the value session, the INTSTAT reassert
 662: +        channel, the TIME pair, and the residual ACCEPT cells).  Every one runs AFTER a refused lb at +0x07, so the
 663: +        latch is SET while the row is measured -- which is the whole point.
 664: +     8  ALREADY PINNED post-latch by an incidental access in an existing session.  Derived, not asserted:
 665: +        opus_close7_20260915/scripts2/session_map2.py (static, no emulator) maps all 45 sessions and finds 72 accepted
 666: +        accesses after their session's first refusal, in 11 sessions, 10 carrying an oracle.

 714: +#
 715: +#  THE ONE STRUCTURAL IDEA, and it is the whole repair: every existing value row is measured
 716: +#  with `d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN' CLEAR, so a guard grown with a conjunct on that latch is
 717: +#  invisible to all of them.  `POST()' below opens each session with a REFUSED lb at +0x07, so
 718: +#  every access after it is measured with the LATCH SET.  New sessions only -- the targeting
 719: +#  rule therefore selects no earlier kill for re-running (no existing session's command list
 720: +#  changes).
 721: +#
 722: +#  CHANNEL lines: each row names its mutant and the observable, and EVERY ONE WAS CHECKED
 723: +#  AGAINST ITS MUTANT rather than merely written -- the seat's first two TIME shapes were
 724: +#  measured VACUOUS and one gave a FALSE RED on a control, which is this block's own evidence

 729: +def POST(A):
 730: +    """A refused lb at +0x07 sets d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN before anything else in the session."""
 731: +    x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v0")
 732: +    return x

 735: +# ---- P1: the REV PROM-probe window, POST.  CHANNEL: pc = ra, which only an ACCEPTED access

 740: +    if op[0] == "s":

 742: +    else:

 744: +    c += x + ["print pc"]
 745: +t, clean = session(c)
 746: +for i, k in enumerate(("sw", "sd", "ld", "lw")):
 747: +    v = nth(t, "pc", i)
 748: +    row("p revP %s pc" % k, "absent" if v is None else "0x%x" % v, "0x%x" % RA,
 749: +        "POST: accepted %s CRIME_REV from the window -> ra" % k)

 771: +t, clean = session(c + ["print s0", "print s1", "print s2", "print s3", "print a1", "print a2"])
 772: +tv = nth(t, "s3", 0)
 773: +row("p acc ctl ld", pr(t, "s0"), "0x100", "POST: accepted ld +0x08 answers CONTROL")

 804: +
 805: +# ---- P4/P5: THE TIME 4-BYTE CELLS, POST, IN THE **GAPPED** SHAPE.  *** THE TWO OBVIOUS SHAPES
 806: +#      WERE MEASURED VACUOUS (post7b.py) and one gave a FALSE RED on a control; only this one
 807: +#      discriminates, at ~13,000x (base 0x13414f8 vs the bypass's 0x5ee). ***  The gap is the
 808: +#      debugger's own pause BETWEEN steps: host time passes, so an update that is performed
 809: +#      arrives before the final 8R and one that is skipped arrives after it.  CHANNEL: a read
 810: +#      takes odata BEFORE its own crime_update_crime_time(). ----
 811: +def gapped_post(kind):
 812: +    ops = [enc("lb", "a3", "t1"),                                            # REFUSED lb -- sets the latch
 813: +           enc("ld", "at", "v1"),                                            # TIME 8R, TIME still ~0
 814: +           enc("sw", "at", "t0") if kind == "w" else enc("lw", "at", "a0"),  # the cell under test
 815: +           enc("ld", "at", "v0")]                                            # what it left behind
 816: +    c = ["at=0x%x" % (CRIME + 0x38), "a3=0x%x" % (CRIME + 0x07), "t0=0x0a0b0c0d",
 817: +         "v0=0x%x" % SENT, "v1=0x%x" % SENT, "a0=0x%x" % SENT, "t1=0x%x" % SENT]
 818: +    for i, op in enumerate(ops):
 819: +        c.append("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, op))
 820: +    c += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
 821: +          "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP), "pc=0x%x" % CODE]
 822: +    c += ["step 2", "step 1", "step 1", "print v0", "print v1"]
 823: +    t, clean = session(c)
 824: +    return nth(t, "v0", 0), nth(t, "v1", 0), (t.count("-- declined.") if clean else "unclean")
 825: +
 826: +
 827: +_gapnote = []
 828: +for kind in ("w", "r"):
 829: +    v0, v1, n = gapped_post(kind)
 830: +    _gapnote.append(n)
 831: +    row("p t4%s gap" % kind, mag(v0), "large",
 832: +        "step2[lb,ld] step1[%s] step1[ld] (raw %s, first ld %s)"
 833: +        % ("sw" if kind == "w" else "lw", None if v0 is None else hex(v0), None if v1 is None else hex(v1)))
 834: +row("p t gap once", _gapnote, [1, 1], "CONTROL: each gapped session announced its one refusal")

 853: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x278, 0x0102030405060708); c += x       # far block 8W
 854: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x278, "t1"); c += x
 855: +t, clean = session(c + ["print s0", "print s1", "print s2", "print a0", "print a1", "print s3",
 856: +                        "print ra", "print a2", "print t1"] + roundtrip(A))
 857: +row("p rev ld", pr(t, "s0"), "0x11", "POST: accepted ld +0x00 answers CRIME_REV")
 858: +row("p rev lw4", pr(t, "s1"), "0x11", "POST: accepted lw +0x04 answers REV low lane")
 859: +row("p dog st+ld", pr(t, "s2"), "0x1122334455667788", "POST: 8W then 8R at CRIME_DOG")
 860: +row("p dog rhi", pr(t, "a0"), "0x11223344", "POST: 4R of the high half (+0x30)")

 862: +row("p dog wlo", pr(t, "s3"), "0x112233440badcafe", "POST: 4W low half lands, high half kept")
 863: +row("p dog whi", pr(t, "ra"), "0xfeedbac0badcafe", "POST: 4W HIGH half lands, low half kept")

 865: +row("p top st+ld", pr(t, "t1"), "0x102030405060708", "POST: 8W then 8R at +0x278")
 866: +row("p resid once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "CONTROL: exactly one refusal (the lb)")

 868: +
 869: +#  *** REGISTER COLLISION, CAUGHT WHILE DRAFTING AND RECORDED RATHER THAN SILENTLY FIXED: the
 870: +#  prototype's residual-survival row reads `v1', which roundtrip() writes.  The DOG 4W-high
 871: +#  readback added here therefore CANNOT use v1 -- it would make one of the two rows read the
 872: +#  other's register and go green for the wrong reason, which is exactly the vacuity this repair

 960:   targeting rule), and I checked that `latch said` reads latch x5's existing transcript only.
 961: * **Pass 7 is owed** because the first VALID pass with zero floor breaches surviving adjudication is not pass 6.  It is
 962:   LIGHT and it VERIFIES rather than hunts: (a) for each of the 46 ACCEPTS cells, name the row that reds under an
 963:   ANNOUNCED decline (`latch said`, by construction) and under a SILENT decline (a value row, or "equivalent", or
 964:   "disclosed") -- 92 cells, derivable by reading; (b) confirm by reading latch x5's command list that every ACCEPTS
 965:   access precedes the lh (the generality argument); (c) nothing else.  Full roster, per the every-stage rule.

1136: ## QUESTIONS -- *** THIS PASS VERIFIES AN ENUMERATION.  IT DOES NOT HUNT CELLS. ***
1137: 
1138: The repair-chain amendment governs this pass, and the flagship ruling repeats it: when two consecutive passes find
1139: breaches of ONE class, the next repair owes the CLASS CLOSURE in one diff, and **the closing pass then VERIFIES THE
1140: ENUMERATION rather than hunting**.  Three passes found this class (pass 6 the announced accept-guard growth, table 10
1141: the width-level latch shrink, pass 7 the per-cell latch shrink); repair 13 is the closure; table 11 measured it.
1142: 
1143: **A BARE ZERO DOES NOT CLOSE THE CHAIN.**  If your answer is "no gap", it must carry its DENOMINATOR -- which cells,
1144: which mutant shapes you swept -- because "zero found" and "zero looked for" must not render the same.
1145: 
1146: 1. **IS THE ENUMERATION ITSELF RIGHT?**  It is `{latch clear, set} x {len 4, 8} x {read, write} x {the SEVEN labels the
1147:    switch distinguishes: REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG, TIME, default}` = 56 cells, read off `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`
1148:    and generated mechanically (the `klatch`/`LABELS` block above emits all 28 latch-SET cells by construction).
1149:    **Is there an AXIS it is missing**, or a label the switch distinguishes that the seven do not cover?  Check it
1150:    against the function, not against my description of the function.
1151: 2. **DOES A CLEAN-SWEEP LATCH-SET MUTANT EXIST THAT TABLE 11 DID NOT BUILD?**  Table 11 built 28 generated cells plus
1152:    K1/K2/K3 and E2: 27 killed, and `C_ctl4r` scored 188/188 because the contract calls CONTROL 4R EQUIVALENT ("reads 0
1153:    either way") -- that is the enumeration's own negative control passing.  Give an exact edit to
1154:    `src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c` that scores **188/188**, and name the row you expected to catch it.
1155: 3. **IS THE PER-CELL DISPOSITION HONEST?**  The contract splits the 46 ACCEPTS entries as **23 ROWED / 8 ALREADY PINNED
1156:    post-latch / 10 EQUIVALENT / 5 HOST-DIAGNOSTIC (clause-(e), disclosed)**.  The 8 come from a STATIC session map
1157:    (`opus_close7_20260915/scripts2/session_map2.py`), not from a measurement.  **Is any cell in the wrong bucket?**  A
1158:    cell called EQUIVALENT that a guest can observe is a floor breach; a cell called ROWED whose row cannot vary under
1159:    its own mutant is vacuous.

1165:    recorded rather than hidden: PRETABLE 11 predicted K2 would red FOUR `p revP * pc` rows and it reds TWO, because K2

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""session_map2.py -- #529 closing pass 7 (Opus, finishing seat).  As session_map.py, plus the ROW NAMES each
session feeds, so `which rows can observe a post-latch accepted access' is DERIVED from the shipped probe rather
than asserted.  Does not run the emulator.  Sentinel: SESSIONMAP_DONE."""
import io, re, sys, contextlib

SRC = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/crime_width_probe.py"
src = open(SRC).read()
src = src.replace("t, clean = session(", "t, clean = _rec(")
assert "t, clean = session(" not in src
src = re.sub(r"(?m)^(\s*)row\(", lambda m: m.group(1) + "_row(", src)
assert re.search(r"(?m)^\s*row\(", src) is None

OPC = {0x20: ("lb", 1, "R"), 0x21: ("lh", 2, "R"), 0x23: ("lw", 4, "R"), 0x24: ("lbu", 1, "R"),
       0x25: ("lhu", 2, "R"), 0x37: ("ld", 8, "R"), 0x28: ("sb", 1, "W"), 0x29: ("sh", 2, "W"),
       0x2b: ("sw", 4, "W"), 0x3f: ("sd", 8, "W")}
CRIME = 0xb4000000
SESSIONS = []
PEND = []


def _row(name, got, want, note=""):
    PEND.append(name)


def _rec(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
    regs, acc, pc = {}, [], None
    for c in cmds:
        m = re.match(r"^([a-z0-9]+)=0x([0-9a-f]+)$", c)
        if m:
            if m.group(1) == "pc":
                pc = int(m.group(2), 16)
            else:
                regs[m.group(1)] = int(m.group(2), 16)
            continue
        m = re.match(r"^put w 0x([0-9a-f]+), 0x([0-9a-f]{8})$", c)
        if m:
            w = int(m.group(2), 16)
            op, rs = w >> 26, (w >> 21) & 0x1f
            if op in OPC and rs in (1, 7):
                base = regs.get("at" if rs == 1 else "a3")
                if base is not None:
                    acc.append((OPC[op], base, int(m.group(1), 16)))
    SESSIONS.append([acc, list(PEND)])          # rows of the PREVIOUS session, flushed here
    del PEND[:]
    return "", True


sys.argv = ["crime_width_probe.py", "/nonexistent-binary", "map"]
buf = io.StringIO()
try:
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buf):
        exec(compile(src, SRC, "exec"), {"__name__": "__main__", "_rec": _rec, "_row": _row})
except SystemExit:
    pass
SESSIONS.append([[], list(PEND)])               # the last session's rows

print("SESSIONS=%d" % (len(SESSIONS) - 1))
tot = 0
for i in range(len(SESSIONS) - 1):
    acc = SESSIONS[i][0]
    myrows = SESSIONS[i + 1][1]
    ref = None
    for j, ((op, ln, d), base, where) in enumerate(acc):
        if ln in (1, 2):
            ref = j
            break
    post = []
    if ref is not None:
        for (op, ln, d), base, where in acc[ref + 1:]:
            if ln in (4, 8) and (base & ~0xfff) == CRIME:
                post.append("%s%s+0x%x" % (op, "P" if (where & 0xffffff00) == 0xbfc05100 else "", base - CRIME))
    tot += len(post)
    if post:
        print("#%-3d acc=%-3d firstRefusal=%-4s POST=%d  %s" % (i, len(acc), ref, len(post), " ".join(post)))
        print("      rows: %s" % ", ".join(myrows))
print("TOTAL post-latch accepted CRIME accesses: %d" % tot)
print("SESSIONMAP_DONE")

PRETABLE 11 -- #529 crimewidthkill, repair 13 (188 rows).  WRITTEN BEFORE TABLE 11 RUNS; this file's own mtime is the
order proof, which is the same discriminator the oracle-correction rule uses.

SUBJECT   src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c, DEVICE_ACCESS(crime): the width guard that used to end the process.
INSTRUMENT regress/crime_width_probe.py, repair 13.  MEASURED 188/188 CONTROL=OK on the committed build before this
          block was written -- killtable11_20260916/probe_repair13_fix.out, ending CRIMEP_DONE, runner rc=0.
BASELINE  the round's post-fix working set (the fix is UNCOMMITTED).  The clone cmp's dev_sgi_ip32.c against
          GXEMUL-SEC before mutant 1; the base leg rebuilds in the clone and doubles as the freshness proof.

WHY THERE IS A TABLE 11 AT ALL
  Closing pass 7 returned GAP.  The K-CLASS -- an accept guard grown with a conjunct on THIS FIX'S OWN LATCH,
  `&& !(<cell> && (d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))' -- declines SILENTLY at that cell once any refusal has happened, because
  the latch is exactly what suppresses the announce.  K1 (INTMASK 4W), K2 (REV 4-byte), K3 (CONTROL 8R) each scored
  157/157 with a measured guest loss.  Every value row the probe had was measured with the latch CLEAR.
  GRADE: FLOOR, not clause (e) -- FABLE_C5_RULING_20260915.md S-A.7, which OVERTURNED the prior RA3.  The warrant is
  in the fix's own diff: the width dispatch's `else' arm READS d->warned, in a hunk this diff ADDS.
  This is the THIRD consecutive pass to find this one class (pass 6 the announced accept-guard growth; table 10 the
  width-level latch shrink W_8r/W_8w; pass 7 the per-cell latch shrink), so the class-closure amendment applies and
  repair 13 owes the closure in ONE diff, with the contract naming which cells are covered and which are not.

THE ENUMERATION, DERIVED FROM THE HANDLER AND NOT FROM THE MUTANTS
  {latch clear, set} x {len 4, 8} x {read, write} x {the SEVEN labels the switch distinguishes: CRIME_REV,
  CRIME_CONTROL, CRIME_INTSTAT, CRIME_INTMASK, CRIME_DOG, CRIME_TIME, default} = 56 cells.
  The latch-CLEAR half is table 10's twelve S_* silent bypasses, already measured.  The 28 latch-SET cells are this
  table's subject.  The GENERATOR is the enumeration: _scratchpad/mutants529k_draft.py emits all 28 by construction,
  and was validated against the shipped fix at 28/28 find-texts occurring EXACTLY ONCE with every edit balancing.

  *** MUTANTS AND ROWS ARE DIFFERENT UNITS, and the FLOOR is the INTERSECTION, not the larger of the two (the ruling,
  S-A.Q1).  A mutant at an equivalent or already-pinned cell teaches nothing; a row at a cell no mutant reaches is
  unfalsifiable. ***  Over the 46 ACCEPTS entries:
        23  ROWED post-latch by repair 13's `p *' sessions        <- the FLOOR
         8  ALREADY PINNED post-latch by an incidental access, derived in opus_close7_20260915/scripts2/
            session_map2.py (static, no emulator: 45 sessions, 72 post-refusal accepted accesses in 11 sessions,
            10 carrying an oracle)
        10  EQUIVALENT at the guest -- nothing to observe, no row owed or possible
         5  HOST-DIAGNOSTIC only -- clause-(e) debt, DISCLOSED with F1/F1b/F3's measurements
        --
        46  ACCEPTS entries (46 ENTRIES, six KINDS)

SIBLING PARITY
  The width dispatch has three arms: len 8, len 4, and the declining else.  The two ACCEPTING arms are the siblings,
  and they are NOT symmetric in what a latch-keyed shrink costs: the 8-byte arm serves whole-register reads and
  writes, the 4-byte arm serves half-register lanes whose OTHER half must survive.  That asymmetry is why the closure
  carries BOTH widths at every label rather than sampling one -- and why `p dog wlo' and `p dog whi' both exist: a
  4-byte write must land in its own half AND leave the other half alone, and only two rows can say that.
  ASYMMETRY FOUND AND OWED A SENTENCE: the `default' label has no case body, so at that label the two accepting arms
  differ only in what the common tail returns.  Rowed by `p acc 40 sw' / `p acc 40 lw' at +0x40 and `p top st+ld' at
  +0x278; the contract says so.

CLAIM -> ROW
  Every added contract sentence asserting an observable consequence maps to a row or to an explicit no-row sentence:
    "the K-class declines silently after any refusal"            -> every `p *' row (each runs after a refused lb)
    "8 cells are already pinned post-latch"                      -> NO ROW OWED; a derivation, cited to session_map2.py
    "10 cells are EQUIVALENT"                                    -> NO ROW POSSIBLE; stated as such
    "5 cells are host-diagnostic only"                           -> NO ROW; clause-(e), disclosed with F1/F1b/F3
    "TIME 4W/4R is guest-visible"                                -> `p t4w gap' / `p t4r gap'
    "a 4-byte write leaves the other half alone"                 -> `p dog wlo' + `p dog whi'
    "an accepted INTSTAT write reasserts IP2"                    -> `p istw sd' / `p istw sw' (+ two CONTROL rows)

GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD
  GUARD reads: len, writeflag, relative_addr, d->warned.
  BODY reads:  d->reg[], d->warned (the else arm's announce test), and the tail's HARDINT/INTSTAT recompute.
  THEY OVERLAP AT d->warned, AND THAT OVERLAP IS THE WHOLE FINDING -- it is why the latch is a floor axis rather than
  clause (e), and it is why a row measured with the latch clear cannot see a guard conditioned on it.
  DISAGREEING-STATE ROW OWED AND SHIPPED: the `p *' sessions ARE that row set -- they drive the same cells as the
  existing value rows with d->warned in the OPPOSITE state.

CHANNEL -- per row, the mutant it kills and the observable that differs.  *** THIS ROUND IS ITS OWN EVIDENCE THAT THE
CHANNEL LINE MUST BE RUN AND NOT MERELY WRITTEN: the seat that proposed the TIME rows named their channel correctly,
and measured its own first two shapes VACUOUS, one of them producing a FALSE RED on a control. ***
    p revP {sw,sd,ld,lw} pc   K2 (REV 4-byte, latch-keyed)   pc = ra, which only an ACCEPTED access reaches
    p acc imask sw            K1 (INTMASK 4W, latch-keyed)   the stored half reads back; a decline answers 0
    p acc ctl ld              K3 (CONTROL 8R, latch-keyed)   0x100 against 0
    p acc istat ld / istw *   a latch-keyed INTSTAT shrink   the value, and cause.IP2 for the reassert EFFECT
    p t4w gap / p t4r gap     E2 (TIME 4-byte bypass)        a read takes odata BEFORE its own
                                                             crime_update_crime_time(), so a skipped update arrives
                                                             ONE ACCESS LATE: base 0x13414f8 vs 0x5ee, ~13,000x
    p dog {rhi,rlo,wlo,whi}   a latch-keyed DOG shrink       each half independently, both directions
    p acc ctl msg             a CONTROL-write shrink         the `unimplemented' announce COUNT (three writes)
    p * once / p resid surv   ALL of them                    CONTROL: exactly one refusal per session; host alive
